## Animal model

Animal models, also referred to as rolling animals, wheeled animal models, or pull toys were toy wood carvings of animals on wheeled platforms with a string to pull them along.

Description
Animal models could be carved to resemble all manner of animals, as well as some monsters. They typically measured  in length,  tall, and weighed . Internal hinges were used in their crafting that enabled their tails to move, as well as their mouths to open and close, as the models were moved along the ground. Some, like those modeled on a wyvern, had hinged wings that would flap as they were moved.

Animal models that were referred to as "pull toys" had a string attached to them, allowing them to be pulled across the ground.

Availability
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold animal models carved from refined Cormyrean and Sembian woods. The types of animals on offered included cows, dragons, elephants, horses, pegasi, pack mules, and unicorns. At one point the magazine offered rolling beholders, but by the 1370'S DR it had been discontinued. The catalogue recommended the toy for children ages two to five and sold them all at a set price of 1 gold piece.
 The Toysmiths, a toyshop in Ravens Bluff that was owned and operated by the gnome brothers Gunder and Relvan Gaewilder, sold animal models in many of the same styles as Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue. However, the brothers charged different prices for each style of model. Horses for 5 silver pieces, elephants for 6 sp, war horses for 7 sp, pegasi and unicorns for 8 sp, and dragons for 10 sp. Additionally, Toysmiths was open to taking custom orders, which could range in price from 5 to 30 silver pieces.

## Anita Fischer

Anita Fischer was a commoner from the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Anita was a human woman with a tan skin, straight brown hair, and hazel eyes.

History
In the, Anita and Elias Fischer suffered a terrible loss. The couple's seven-year-old daughter Lily was murdered, leaving her parents grief-stricken. Anita and Elias planned on burying their daughter in the Lower City Graveyard, but the undertaker carpenter, Ulkov Sully, demanded a priority fee for a coffin due to a massive backlog of funerals caused by the skyrocketing numbers of murders in the Gate. Without paying the fee, something the Fischers could not do, it would've taken a month of wait time. Anita and her husband tried their best to get Lily buried via negotiations and demands from the Valmorba and her mortuary.

## Antwun Dufay

Antwun Dufay was a vampire spawn chamberlain who worked at the Szarr Palace of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Antwun was a man with sharp features, pale skin, and long straight dark hair, dressed in finery.

History
Chamberlain Dufay entered Cazador Szarr's service some years before 1492 DR. He supervised the entirety of the vampire lord's army of enthralled servants, guests, and hirelings in the Szarr Palace located in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate. He sternly warned the staff of several rules they had to follow: Cazador liked silence and demanded no one speak unless spoken to; he liked diligence, and no servant was to be seen idling by him; Cazador liked to be respected, so all servants were to look down in his presence; and lastly there was to be no whistling. Apart from the staff, Dufay was also in charge of event planning and "Cazador's finest" vampire spawn. The chamberlain ordered the spawn to hunt for lowborn Baldurians only, to avoid the patriars' wrath, especially in the late 15 century DR after Cazador's spawn took members of the Dlusker, Jhasso, and Oathoon families.

In the, Cazador Szarr was ready to perform a ritual that would elevate him to being a vampire ascendant. Chamberlain Dufay ordered no one to be admitted into the palace on the day before the ritual, promising the three guards of the entrance a "reward" that was to come after the ritual's completion. With one of Cazador's vampire spawns missing, however, Antwun Dufay was ordered to take his place in the ritual. However, Dufay knew of what would happen to him in the ritual and made a plan to avoid that fate. He purchased a potion from Bonecloak's Apothecary that would knock the imbiber out, giving Dufay the appearance of death and to make sure the illusion was believable, he also purchased lookalike potions of acid poison.

It was unknown if Antwun Dufay consumed the potion and outlived his master. By the time the ritual began, his body was found in the Szarr Palace, showing evidence of acid poisoning, implying he accidentally drank the wrong vial. Lurianna Sauvage, a werewolf lover of Antwun's, was not informed of the chamberlain's plan as he was unsure if she could be trusted not to betray the plan to her master. When she found her beloved's body, Lurianna wrote a goodbye note and drank another poison potion, following Dufay into the afterlife.

## Animalistic power

Animalistic power was an arcane and divine transmutation spell from the second circle of magic that strengthened the target with an aspect of the natural world. It could be used by a wide variety of spellcasters: clerics,druids, rangers, sorcerers, and wizards.

Effects
With a simple touch, the caster imbued the target with an aspect of natural world, boosting their physical strength, dexterity, and constitution. The spell's effect was apparent from a sparkling green glow that momentarily surrounded the target creature. Once the glow was gone, the target creature appeared more feral and bigger in size.

Components
The spell required a verbal, somatic, and material components. Its material component was a tuft of beast fur, a handful of feathers, or a chunk of skin.

## Anazar Narroun

Anazar Narroun was a priest of Amaunator from the town of Eveningstar in Cormyr in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Anazar was a human man with sharp features, light skin, and black hair gathered in a tight ponytail. He wore robes of Amaunator's clergy.

History

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar in the mid-1480s DR, Anazar Narroun helped to organize a group of Evenors as they responded to emerging crises. While Derevon Ybir wanted all efforts devoted to repairing the town's barricades, he prioritized the priestess Meena Follakin and her request to to investigate a strange disease that had emerged in Eveningstar's War Hospital, transforming infected men and women into red musk zombies. He sent a team of adventurers to aid her, which quickly put an end to Peron Uthe's zombie rampage. He then directed the team to Eolynn Arva and the threat at Uthe Lodge, which saved Eveningstar as a whole from the dryad Sylvie.

The last excursion put the adventurers in contact with the Harpers through Jussko Kwahn and his fellows Kirinel Zay and Tarim Inavule, who were posing as concerned and unusually-capable travelers. Anazar was happy to let these conspicuous do-gooders help in tracking down the once-beloved druid Halsaime at Thorngap, first to Thorn Hollow and then the Druid's Deep. By the combined efforts of everyone involved, Halsaime was finally cornered at the Deep and killed, and his evil artifact of a staff destroyed.

In the late 15 century DR, Antone and his master Belvedere asked adventurers to find and assist their young charge, the nobleman adventurer and relic collector Oriphaun Huntsilver. To their endless consternation and concern, Oriphaun found himself at the heart of a plot to attack the city of Wheloon by the shadowy forces of Returned Netheril. Afterwards, they tracked him as he got entangled with the druids of Druid's Deep, walked the High Road with the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll, and disappeared into the Storm Horns to fight the Shadovar.

## Anatomist

Anatomists were a subtype of necromancer that specialized in a focus on the physical sciences, devoting themselves to the scientific study of bodies.

Culture
Those who traversed this profession typically went down one of two developmental routes. Most went down this path to further dark motives, becoming either "mad scientists" that created horrid experiments or experts in torturing that were sometimes employed by despots. These horrible experiments could lead to the creation of various types of golems, such as flesh golems and necrophidius. But some who adopted the life of an anatomist did so for benign purposes, wanting to serve society as skilled healers and physicians.

Out of all types of necromancers, anatomists were the ones most tied to civilization, as large towns and cities were the only places one could ensure a steady and safely procured supply of cadavers. Although, those of the torturer bent tended to practice on living subjects instead of cadavers. This was vital for any anatomist, as going longer than a month without dissecting a body would risk their skills atrophying.

As some civilizations held taboos and stigmas with corpse dissection, anatomists often had to keep their experiments a secret. They would masquerade as "ordinary" doctors or non-necromancer wizards that had gained their knowledge from attending academic lectures or reading ancient text, rather than from first-hand experience. To better circumvent around these restrictions many anatomists worked together under a secret organization known as the Anatomical Academy. This group helped to serve anatomists, as well as any non-necromancers with interest in anatomy, through the collection and spreading of both necromantic knowledge of anatomy and fresh cadavers.

Abilities
Above all the fields of study, anatomists were always skilled in anatomy and healing, acquired over many years of extensive study, making them far better doctors than most village healers. Because of their expertise in anatomy they were also quite skilled at determining a corpse's time and cause of death through performance of an autopsy.

Some anatomists were also known to have skills in herbalism, venom handling, and various aspects of dealing with animals. Some were also skilled in the rogueish arts of disguise and forgery out of necessity to better mask their life as anatomists. They rarely ever showed interest in netherworld knowledge or the creation and control of undead, though sometimes found the latter useful for manual labor.

In terms of spells, anatomists preferred those of the necromancy school whose effects directly affected physical bodies, either prior to or following death, often using magic to aid or better inform their research. This included such spells as bone growth, corpse link, delay death, embalm, empathic wound transfer, and graft flesh. They often hesitated to employ offensive magics, as they might risk making a corpse unsuitable for later study.

Possessions
Anatomists were typically garbed in the mantle and robes of either a physician or scholar. They usually carried around their surgical tools, keeping them in a black leather bag or a small box. These surgical tools included such things as bonesaws, knives, retractors, and scalpels.

Due to their surgical precision, they preferred fighting with small edged or cutting weapons, starting with a simple knife. Later on they would take to handling such weapons as daggers, darts, cutlasses, and short swords.

Notable Anatomists
 Danna the Skull, an anatomist that worked for the Forthright Band of Investigators.
 Ellandra Tolbert, a famous Neveren anatomist. She was born to an equally famous anatomist father, whom she assisted with dissections in her youth, and she was a member of the Anatomical Academy.

## Andrui's baneful backfire

Andrui's baneful backfire was an abjuration spell from the 5 circle of magic invented by Andrui of Oerth and designed to protect a wizard's active enchantments and magical items.

Effects
The spell's target was an active ensorcellment, be it magic made permanent or a creature temporarily slowed down, or an enchanted item located within the caster's eyesight. After being cast, Andrui's baneful backfire remained dormant until an attempt was made to magically tamper or dispel the spell's target. Firstly, once triggered, baneful backfire automatically caused all attempts to dispel magic to fail. The shock caused by that failure also burned a single spell out of the caster's memory. The lost spell was of an equal level as the spell that was being dispelled. If the caster did not have a spell of such level in their memory, the baneful backfire burned a spell of the next lower circle of magic. The spell's only limitation was the power of its caster. Only a spellcaster more powerful than the mage who placed Andrui's baneful backfire could dispel the enchantment in question as well as the baneful backfire cast upon it.

Components
Andrui's baneful backfire required verbal, somatic, and material components to be cast. Its material components were a square 3-inch (7.6 centimeters) piece of carpet and a small clump of cat hair.

History
In the late 14 century DR, the Wail of the Banshee, a ship made of bone that belonged to Mezzalcat, a drow necromancer, was protected by the Andrui's baneful backfire spell. The ship was docked in Tradesport, an island fortress in the Sea of Endless Darkness in the Underdark underneath the Vast.

## Animal telepathy

Animal telepathy was a minor psionic devotion that allowed a psionicist to telepathically communicate with various broad ranges of living creatures. Some might refer to it as being a more limited version of mindlink.Though this power was never formally updated for 2 edition AD&D, it was sporadically mentioned in some sources. Encyclopedia Magica Volume II's description of discipline requirements for an item originally published in Dragon #91 (page 540), Monstrous Compendium Annual Volume Four'''s entry on the brain coral creature that was originally published in Dragon #116, and in Polyhedron #49's "The Living City: The For-Rest Inn" (page 29). Of these three, Monstrous Compendium Annual Volume Four addresses the power in relation to 2 edition psionics, referring to it as a limitation on the brain corals' use of the devotion mindlink.

Effects
The types of living things that could be communicated with through animal telepathy, as well as power's operational range and the range at which creatures could be searched for, depended upon the manifester's degree of mastery with the devotion and their psionic powers as a whole. It offered psionicists no greater ability to command or influence the minds of such creatures. The devotion operated as follows:
Level of MasteryTypes of Animal Life which can be Communicated withFirst DegreeMammalsThird DegreeMarsupialsFifth DegreeAviansSixth DegreeReptilesSeventh DegreeAmphibiansEight DegreeFish and similar aquatic creaturesTenth DegreeArachnids, centipedes, and millipedesTwelfth DegreeCreatures traditionally considered "monsters"Fourteenth DegreePlants

Notable Users
 Gypsy psionicists often learned this devotion.
Creatures & Sentient Races
 Baku, a powerful psionic race, innately possessed a 12 degree mastery of animal telepathy.
 Brain coral, an intelligent and psionic range of coral, innately possessed a 9 degree mastery of animal telepathy.
 Planetars, one of the orders of angels in the multiverse, innately possessed a 14 degree mastery of animal telepathy.
 Solars, another order of angels in the multiverse, innately possessed a 4 degree mastery of animal telepathy''.

Individuals
 Elrem the Wise, a powerful Uthgar shaman of the Great Worm tribe.The North: Guide to the Savage Frontier and Powers & Pantheons both state that the "great worm" form an Uthgar shaman can shapechange into confers a number of psionic powers. The powers listed are nearly the same as those that were originally detailed for Elrem back in The Savage Frontier, thus it can be inferred that Elrem's psionic powers are not unique to him. The only power absent in both sources is animal telepathy. This is presumably due to it not having made the transition from 1 to 2 edition AD&D, rather than it being unique to Elrem in any way. Therefore, it can be further inferred that animal telepathy is likewise a power this "great worm" form confers.
 Illistyl Elventree, a member of the Knights of Myth Drannor.
 Peter Trum.

## Ancestral defender

An ancestral defender was a ghostly manifestation of a noble Ffolk warrior that could be summoned into battle by the upper echelons of the Callidyrr dragoons.

Description
Ancestral defenders appeared when the Ffolk spirits that empowered a dragoon were stirred into action.

Combat
The spirit could not attack the dragoon's foes except by redirecting their attacks back at them. A defender's primary role in battle was to assist or heal a dragoon and their allies, however it could not act independently: the dragoon had to direct and choose its actions, and even the spirit's ability to exist on the battlefield was tied to the dragoon's own vitality.

Society
The spirit could be summoned using a primal rite known as a dragoon summons. A Callidyrr dragoon focused their willpower and howled an ancient, traditional Ffolk battle cry to summon the spirit, which appeared within  of the dragoon and was heralded by a chill wind. An ancestral defender lingered until it was destroyed or until the summoner dismissed it.

## Antinia

Antinia the Goon was an unsavory element that resided in the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Antinia was a human woman with a light skin tone, black eyes, and long straight black hair gathered in a ponytail. She was a proficient fighter who wore a simple breastplate and armed herself with two axes.

History
In the, was part of the same gang with Gal Vharranne, Churg Elvek, Dart Dirtwood, Imprudence, Nobster, and Ormo who could be found in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate near the docks where the Steel Watch Foundry and Water Queen's House stood. Unbeknownst to most, her gang's leader, Churg, was a True Soul, an individual implanted with an illithid tadpole that indoctrinated him into serving the Absolute. Under the Cult of the Absolute's bidding, Antinia and her gang captured the famed writer and traveler Volothamp Geddarm and set to publically execute him for spreading "lies and conspiracies" – talking about the Cult. The gang was confronted by the Absolute Adversaries, who attempted to save the master thespian. It was unknown if Antinia and her gang survived the encounter.

## Ankhapur/Sewers

The sewers of Ankhapur were a sanitation system beneath the eponymous Arnaden port city of the same name.

Structure
Surface Connections
 In a disused and otherwise a dead-end portion of the sewers, that was teeming with wildlife and not far from Ankhapur's docks, was a portal set up by the Moonstars as part of their network of portals for ease of access across the continent of Faerûn.

Notable Inhabitants
 A grick of abnormally large size and intelligence, distinguished by a bright green beak, made its home in these sewers. It was on good terms with the Moonstars and would not attack anyone coming through or into the area the portal was in, a portion of the sewers that the grick claimed as part of its hunting ground. Though recognizing that the Moonstars were "not food", the grick would not endeavor to provide them any assistance or aid. Likewise, Moonstar agents that utilized the portal were warned not to attack the grick.

History
In the, the Turmish Emperor at the time, Shoon VII, took drastic measures ridding the cities of Ankhapur, Calimport, Innarlith, and Ithmong of their criminal underworlds. He ordered oil to be poured into their respective sewers, causing the Great Fires that destroyed the four cities nearly completely, with a deathtoll numbering over three million people.

In the late 15 century DR, Ankhapur was one among many cities that raise or instate new taxes in order to pay for civic utilities such as its sewer, defensive walls, public pumps and troughs, and streets.

## Animal Lords

The Animal Lords were near-godlike spirits inhabiting the Beastlands that had dominion over all animals of their type across the Prime Material and Upper Planes.

The Animal Lords were anthropomorphic representations of their animal kinds, as animals in humanoid shape. There was a lord for every kind of natural animal. Thus, in the Beastlands lived countless animal lords. Each Animal lord had two possible shapes: that of an animal and that of a beautiful humanoid with subtle characteristics of the lord's animal.

Animal lords were immune to charm effects and they had innate telepathy and the ability to foresee perils to a certain degree.

## Andromalius

Earl Andromalius was a devil from Acheron who served Bane. He was involved with, and killed as part of, the early stages of the pixelating curse before it was brought to Toril in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Andromalius looked repulsive.

Abilities
As the ruler of Cube 1717, Andromalius was the only one who had the power to allow teleportation from the cube. When he died, this meant nobody could teleport from the cube.

Activities
In Cube 1717, Andromalius performed magical experiments on those that he had imprisoned.

Relationships
Andromalius was served by Avnas, a derro from Acheron. He considered her a valuable asset for her abilities in engineering. He gave her the book of patterns, which ultimately led to the pixelating curse.

History
While Andromalius ruled Cube 1717, three adventurers tried and failed to overthrow him. They were individually charged and then their heads were cut off and merged together into one body. The three were brought back to life as one being: the skull lord Vargo. Andromalius then imprisoned Vargo in Cube 1717's prisons for 1 million life sentences.

Hundreds of years later, his servants discovered a quadrone from Mechanus. It was given to his servant Avnas to perform experiments on. This transformed the quadrone into the Archquadrone, who then created the pixelating curse. The curse spread quickly through Cube 1717 and caused a conflict in which Andromalius was slain.

After his death, Vargo, now the ruler of Cube 1717, kept Andromalius's head in a case made of glass. The head was pixelated by the pixelating curse, and rotten.

In, she was impersonating Namra Dunseltree.

Relationships
While in her impersonation, she was under the direct command of Daerdatha, another drow sorceress, who controlled her by direct mental commands on occassion. She worked together with a fellow impersonator who played the part of Inder Dunseltree, though he did not give his name.

Unknown to her, more than one person could affect her mind through the spells of Daerdatha, including the warrior Quztyr, whom she was acquainted with.

History
Anlaervrith Mrantarr had been an initiate of Lolth in an undisclosed subterranean city until she was contacted by the sorceress Daerdratha, who recruited her for an infiltration. In the infilrtation, she was required to take the shape of Namra Dunseltree, the wife of a merchant from Scornubel; she played her part to the best of her ability, taking the opportunity to indulge her appetites, both for food, and for cruelty. She received many commands directly in her mind.

At some point, Anlaervrith whipped one of her servants, a young woman by the name of Isryl, to near death; finding her walking again not a day later, Anlaervrith attacked her, still in the guise of Namra. Unknown to her, that was actually Qilué Veladorn, who responded by mind-touching her, stealing a handful of memories, knocking her out, disabling her transformation, and then whisking her away to the vicinity of the Ladystone in Waterdeep.

Aftermath
Qilué took her shape and infiltrated her next mission, not being discovered until later.

## Ana Brabener

Ana Brabener was a peasant girl of Eveningstar, Cormyr, in the late 15 century DR who was unknowingly the Thread of the Weave, a living repository of Mystra's divinity.

History

Ana was a weave anchor, unknowingly imbued with the power of Mystra, Goddess of Magic. However, the origin of her arcane powers was unknown. One of the prevailing theories amongst Elminster and the Wizards of War after it became known that she was the Thread of the Weave was that when the Spellplague struck and the Weave was destroyed, a fragment of the Weave was unstuck in time and space and attached itself to Ana.

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar in the mid-1480s DR, Ana was captured by the drow along with several other villagers of Eveningstar during their hunt for the Thread of the Weave and held in the Eveningstar Cavern. As the captives were about to be dragged into the Underdark, adventurers from Eberron emerged into the cavern from the portal to the Underdark and slew Ydrae Avithoul and the rest of Ana's drow captors, freeing her and the other villagers.

Although Ana and her friend Cormul Haythorne had initially decreed it to be too dangerous, they and their friend Torin Betrich made plans to find the Thread on their own and sought out an old Temple of Mystra deep in the King's Forest. However, the three were ambushed by the drow when they neared the temple. At that moment, Ana manifested the power of the Weave, resulting in an explosion that killed several of the drow and knocked out Cormul and Torin. Due to her strange display of magic, she was captured by Odezzt Avithoul and taken deep into the temple, where the taskmaster sought to discover how it was that she had cast that spell. After they recovered, Cormul returned to Eveningstar to find help while Torin remained in the woods to search for Ana.

At Corin's behest, adventurers delved into the Temple of Mystra in search of Ana, where they found her and several other villagers enslaved and collared by the drow. Ana was forced to fight her saviors, though Odezzt's death broke his control over her and she was then freed from her collar. With her newfound powers, she destroyed the magical barriers erected by the drow to prevent their escape. Upon returning to Eveningstar, she was taken in by Elminster as his student so that she could better use her magical talents, if only to protect the village.

During the Battle for Eveningstar, Ana held the gates of the town while Elminster drew the drow forces away as a distraction and adventurers pressed into the drow lines to destroy their field headquarters. With the drow in full retreat, adventurers pursued them into Sschindylryn, striking at the three main noble houses and the Sschindylryn Ziggurat to stop the High Priestess of Lolth, Hekta Szind, from scrying the location of the Thread of the Weave. When the time came to attack the Temple of Lolth, Ana and Elminster joined the adventurers, with Ana confronting the High Priestess along with them while Elminster maintained the portal between the Temple and the Ziggurat. However, they were too late to stop the divination ritual, and the drow discovered that the Thread of the Weave was in fact Ana. She was then mind controlled into walking through a portal to the Demonweb Pits, giving the prize to Lolth.

Ana was taken to Lolth, who held her captive to draw on the Thread of the Weave's vast arcane power as part of her bid to become the next Goddess of Magic. Ana's power was bound into several orbs of Lolth that Lolth dispersed around the Demonweb. Elminster's allies performed one final attack to sabotage Lolth's plans and rescue Ana. With three of the orbs removed, the bindings placed on her were weakened, allowing Ana to regain enough of her power that rescuing her became a possibility. Breaking in without stealth or guile, the heroes fended off the armies of minions and Lolth herself as they freed Ana and helped her drain more orbs of Lolth, which strengthened her further while Lolth raged. Finally, the rescue party found a functioning portal out of the Demonweb Pits and escaped with Ana, emerging in the Underdark of Faerûn. Lolth appeared one final time in material reality to smite Ana as a lesson in power—only to find that she could not, and Ana blasted the Demon Queen back into her domain.

Knowing that Lolth would continue to pursue the Thread of the Weave and place Eveningstar in more danger, Ana and Elminster departed together in order to hide her from Lolth's gaze, and so that Elminster could continue teaching her so that she could master her newfound power.

## Andras

Andras was a Weavespinner in the mid-14 century DR.

History
Andras was present in the aftermath of High Lady Alustriel's fight against the Weave-wraiths from Jethaere during the, at the same time that Shandril Shessair was on a caravan headed for Waterdeep. He assisted Alustriel in the aftermath, learning the Weave-wraiths were riding her, with her consent, and that she was binding them. Attending to her during a bath, after handing her rosefire tea, he was told that Mystra had forbid the outright destruction of the Weave-wraiths so long as they moved not against mages, took away anyone's free will, or damaged wards. He also learned that Alustriel planned to call on the help of Shandril's spellfire to better bind them later, but that she also believed it may be necessary to summon Elminster and Khelben to destroy them if she faltered before that was possible. To his horror, she suggested smashing the Altar of Mystra to draw their attention if they didn't pay attention to spell-callings.

## Anauviir

Anauviir was a soldier in Athalantar under the command of Helm Stoneblade, who followed him into banditry, through the early-to-mid 3 century DR.

Appearance
He was stubbled.

Personality
He was especially restless and aggressive amongst Helm's men, the last to kneel, and yet the first to question whether their protections were sufficient.

Abilities
Anauviir was quite swift, and truly deadly with a dagger.

Possessions
He owned at least one dagger as of .

Activities
Until, Anauviir was one of the bandits that were loyal to the Stag Throne of Athalantar while in insurrection against the Magelords.

Relationships
He was a soldier under Helm Stoneblade.

History
When in 240 DR, Helm Stoneblade found a scrying crystal, and Halidar among his men attempted to escape upon appearing to recognize the device, it was Anauviir that struck that man down. He was the last to kneel when approached by the elf Ruvaen and the prince Elminster Aumar.

During the siege proper, he accompanied Helm Stoneblade as they were led by Farl, a local underworld personality, who knew of a secret passage that allowed entry to the castle through the sewers and ultimately the jakes. He crawled through the pipe leading up to Briost Elthauryn's chambers.

He continued to fight through the siege, making a last stand at some point together with Helm and Darrigo Trumpettower.

Anauviir survived that point in the confrontation, and during the subsequent coronation, he questioned why Myrjala Talithyn, a sorceress who helped during the fight, did not kneel before the new king. She directed him to ask Mithtyn.

## Annathe

Annathe was a member of the Moonclaws gang of Athalantar during the 3 century DR.

History
Annathe participated in the Moonclaws's burglary of the Trumpettower home after the wedding of Peeryst Trumpettower and Nanue Glarmeir in the . However, a rival gang, the Velvet Hands, attempted the same burglary at the same time and Darrigo Trumpettower intervened and fought them. In the aftermath of the ensuing struggle, Farl, leader of the Velvet Hands, accounted for Annathe and others of the Moonclaws as almost certainly lost (whether he meant dead, injured, or captured was not explicit), thus thinning the numbers of the Moonclaws.

## Antaerl

Antaerl was a figure of the criminal underworld in Hastarl during the early 3 century DR.

Relationships
He was acquainted with Farl, a local thief, and Shildo, a local thug.

Rumors & Legends
In, the thief Farl of Hastarl fooled local thug Shildo into leaving a new arrival to the city, Eladar (an assumed identity of prince Elminster Aumar) alone by claiming that Eladar was obviously enspelled by Neldryn Hawklyn already. When Shildo said no one would know, Farl said Antaerl had just left to explain the situation, leaving it to Farl to dissuade Shildo because of some debt Antaerl owed him. This persuaded Shildo.

## Ansildabar's Last Rest

Ansildabar's Last Rest was a crypt in Hastarl, Athalantar, during the 3 century DR.

Location
The tomb was in a walled-off burial ground across the city from the Wanton Wench, a tavern.

Structure
The crypt had a sloping roof, which was shaded by two trees. The burial ground was largely in disrepair, with trees growing through some of the buildings.

Interior
There was a coffin inside the crypt.

Defenses
Other than being walled off, the burial ground was left completely unguarded.

History
At some point, the crypt had held the mortal remains of Ansildabar, a famous explorer.

Ansildabar's Last Rest was already extant by the, when Prince Elminster Aumar took the alias of Eladar the Dark to become a thief of Hastarl. The prince, and his fellow thief Farl, a local criminal, used the tomb to hide away from the local militia after they greatly offended Thelorn Selemban and Jansibal Otharr, two local noble heirs.

At a later date, Elminster used the tomb for the remains of Tassabra, another fellow thief, evicting Ansildabar's remains to do so.

## Ancient gemstone

The ancient gemstone was a magical item similar to an Eberronnian warforged docent, recovered from the Temple of Mystra in the King's Forest in the mid-1480s DR.

Description
The "gemstone" was actually a sphere studded with metal and gemstones. Seemingly intended for use as a power source for a magical device, it could also be slotted into a warforged.

Powers
The ancient gemstone gave the bearer improved resilience and fortitude, and even seemed to twist luck in their favor.

History
During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Cormyrean forces from Eveningstar searched the Temple of Mystra for the lost Ana Brabener, who had been captured by the fell Eldrin Szind, Odezzt Avithoul and Phargred Avithoul. In the process they also found this peculiar item.

## Ansel

Ansel was an human cleric student of the Neverwinter Academy in Neverwinter in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Ansel was a human man with a light skin tone and light hair the color of hay. He wore simple robes of an acolyte.

Relationships
Ansel attended the same school as the person who would become the Hero of Neverwinter. He studied divine magic and healing under Elynwyd, the instructor in charge of the school's clerics. Ansel was a deeply devote to his deity and believed only the divine could cure the plague that gripped the city and that Lady Aribeth de Tylmarande's attempts to stop the disease were futile and would only prolong the suffering.

History
When the Wailing Death struck the city of Neverwinter in the, Ansel resided and trained in the Neverwinter Academy, honing his magical and healing skills. However, his graduation was not to be—Ansel's life was cut short in the attack on the Academy that left most of its staff and students dead and the school itself put to the torch.

## Anechu

Anechu was a village in the Ausa Province of T'u Lung. According to Tan Hikao Nong, it was a prosperous village whose inhabitants enjoyed a high quality of life unusual within T'u Lung, and it was for that reason that he considered it one of his favorite places.

Geography
It was situated along the northern bank of the upper Akensai River, where said river converged with an unnamed tributary. It was connected by road to the city of Chempka upstream and by trail to the villages of Honan and Chin as well as the city of Keeling.

Government
As of, Anechu was led by Ho Kui Dung, a principled and trustworthy man who was a member of the same Ho clan that ruled over Ausa Province as a whole.

Notable Locations
The Hearth Warmth, an inn which Tan Hikao Nong considered to serve the finest food within T'u Lung, was situated within Anechu.

Anechu was also home to a temple which housed a golden statue of Ju Shou, the god of the west. As Ju Shou had aided him before, Tan Hikao Nong frequently made offerings to him at this temple.

## Antarn

Antarn the Sage was a sage from Athkatla, Amn, during the 14 century DR. He was best known for The High History of Faerûnian Archmages Mighty.
{{quote|It is written by some that Elminster failed the test, and was destroyed by Mystra and replaced by another who took the name and face of the last Aumar of Athalantar. Others say Elminster went mad, and is mad yet. Still others affirm that he passed the test, and turned away from the divinity that might have been his, and so became the wise but twisted thing he is today...|Antarn the Sage, in The High History of Faerûnian Archmages Mighty}}

Description
A snappy dresser, the man was short and slender, with a beard he dyed chestnut, to retain its natural color.

Personality
A lifelong loner who'd devoted himself to study and scrivening, he responded to danger with fatalism. Only book burning would enrage him, but rather than being shaken, he'd plot doom inwardly. The man also liked highcoin ladies-of-the-evening, and board games as foreplay.

Abilities
He was a sage in matters historical, local, royal, and arcane, whose knowledge of actual spells was greater than he was willing to admit to.

Possessions
He was habitually protected by an ironguard ring and a ring of shooting stars. Almost all of his time he spent in a tower on the east side of the city.

Activities
Antarn copied books for numerous Athkatlan thieves, who also supplied him with rare examples. On the occasions he went out of his tower, he bought the provender with which he cooked for his two live-in assistants. He was most often seen and identified when the three of them went to Taerandamlur's feasthouse for early dinners; this was the way the sage normally took clients. He also reported to clients through these same dinners.

More rarely, he strolled out to see the sights, and to engage the services of high-coin ladies-of-the-evening.

Relationships
He was attended by Hyelan Zorandel and Drethtar Wildtree, two retired adventuring warriors who served as his assistants, go-betweens, trade agents, and errand-runners. They were also his only friends. Through them, he had deals with other book-thieves of the city.

History
Though his best-known work was The High History of Faerûnian Archmages Mighty, published in the, and touching strongly on the subject of Elminster Aumar. This work was notable for it being a known record of certain less-known events in Elminster's life, such as his time in the laying of the Mythal'' of Myth Drannor, and him having been offered divinity in the .

Antarn was also the author of Dursunder Street: A Local History, and Huntsilver Errant: A Life of Ondamarr Huntsilver.

By, he had become an aging recluse.

## Annicole

Annicole Meloura was the wife of a trader from the Demiplane of Dread. He cursed her with his dying breath because of her unfaithfulness.

Relationships
She was married to Yassad Meloura.

History
While the trader she was married to, Yassad, went on long trips, Annicole had taken a lover without his knowledge. One day he returned home, he caught the two of them in bed; her lover and husband fought to the death of both, and then her husband pronounced a death curse on her with his last words, which turned her into a werespider. She fled to a nearby cave; the unborn child she carried was turned into a morass of spiders, bearing her curse. At some point, a bear entered the cave, and she fought it to the death.

Legacy
The spiders that had once been her unborn child remained inside the cave, alive; they behaved as normal spiders, but their bite carried lycanthropy. At some later date, they bit Sundian Silgathen and Jebbrie Vokasich, turning the first into a werespider and placing the latter in life-threatening danger.

## Animated Series Handbook

The Animated Series Handbook is a sourcebook by Matthew Sernett that adapts the characters of the Dungeons & Dragons cartoon series of 1983–1985 to the 3.5-edition of . It was published by Wizards of the Coast and released with the Dungeons & Dragons – The Complete Animated Series DVD boxset on December 5, 2006. It presents stats of the main characters; a mini-adventure, "Beneath the Blade of Sword Mountain," designed to lead into the Season 2 episode "The Dragon's Graveyard;" and a map of the Realm (no relation to the Forgotten Realms).

## Anatomical Academy

The Anatomical Academy was a secret society dedicated to aiding and advancing the field of anatomical study, spreading detailed knowledge of the body and providing its members cadavers for their studies, even in societies where their field was considered taboo or even outlawed.

Activities
The Anatomical Academy mission was centered around two things: providing members cadavers and facilitating the collection and dissemination of necromantic knowledge. Though necromancy had a reputation as the "Dark Art" among the schools of magic, this was a purely neutral organization, engaging with the necromantic from an analytical and scholarly perspective.

In whatever society they had roots in, the Anatomical Academy commanded an established network of contacts among local authorities. These were typically individuals that worked at cemeteries, morgues, prisons, and poorhouses. From these the Academy sourced some of their cadavers. They also frequently hired adventurers and rogues to establish connections with new sources of cadavers or to transport large shipments of bodies to subscribing members who lived out in secluded places. Cadavers were usually shipped across large distances by ship or wagon.

The Anatomical Academy generally didn't inquire as to the source of the bodies they paid for, with some members of a less-ethical bent merely caring that a specimen be "essentially intact." But other members, being more conscientious practitioners of anatomical study, were committed to only practicing upon corpses that had died a natural death.

Prior to being transported, cadavers would be magically preserved by a field agent through use of the spell embalm. Under dire circumstances, a field agent would resort to animating cadavers and disguising them. However, such a method could carry its own risk of attracting unwanted attention.

Base of Operations
The organization was not known to have a central base of operations. Rather, its members typically gathered at secluded or abandoned locations for any conventions or secret lectures, places such as dungeons, warehouses, or even university classrooms that were not seeing use.

Cities they were known to operate in included Neverwinter and Waterdeep.

Tactics
Being a secret society, this order strove to keep a low profile. They had no desire to risk facing condemnation or persecution from the more conservative and lawful good elements of Faerûn that frowned upon the mutilation of cadavers.

Members of the Anatomical Academy maintained steady contact with one another through discrete courier services, the use of certain spells, and enchanted devices.

Members
Whilst the bulk of the organization was composed of anatomists, the Anatomical Academy was not exclusive to necromancers. It was open to anyone that possessed knowledge of or an interest in anatomy. This included all other manner of wizards, priests, healers, and surgeons.

Notable Members
 Doctor Ellandra Tolbert, a wealthy Neverwintan anatomist.
 Matanzas, a Loviatan priest who had vivisection facilities in his estate that were sometimes used as a meeting place for the Academy.
